I just don't want to go the other direction.\n\nDoctor: Have you been deliberately eating less in order to keep your weight where it is?\n\nPatient: Yes, I have been. On the days I'm in the workshop, I usually just have tea... and maybe a few careful bites of something small. Stopping for a real meal just feels unnecessary to me. Plus, it kind of throws off the control I've got over the day, and my weight. So I just keep it to the bare minimum.\n\nDoctor: Does your body weight play a big part in how you judge yourself as a person?\n\nPatient: I wouldn't say it's how I judge myself as a person, no. I judge myself by my work, by the furniture I build... things like that. My weight is just something I manage. It's about staying disciplined and keeping that control, but it doesn't define who I am.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel fat in any part of your body?\n\nPatient: No, not at all. I don't look at my stomach or my arms and think they're fat. Like I was saying before, I know I'm light. I don't really scrutinize different parts of my body like that... it's just about maintaining the overall control. So, no, I don't feel fat anywhere.\n\nDoctor: Have your menstrual periods stopped?\n\nPatient: No, they haven't stopped... well, I mean, I went through menopause a few years ago. So I don't have periods anymore, but that's just because of my age, not because of anything else.\n\nDoctor: Have you had fainting spells?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't... I've never passed out or had any fainting spells, no. I stay pretty steady on my feet.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your current low weight is medically dangerous?\n\nPatient: No, I don't think it's dangerous. My husband and... well, the apprentices at work, they keep saying I look too thin. But I just tell them I'm fine. I really think people are just confusing discipline with sickness. I'm just disciplined. So no, I don't believe I'm in any medical danger.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever made yourself vomit to keep from gaining weight?\n\nPatient: No, I don't do that. I've never made myself throw up. I just control what I eat. I don't need to do anything like that.\n\nDoctor: What is your current weight?\n\nPatient: I'm not exactly sure right now. I don't really weigh myself... I just haven't stepped on a scale lately. I mostly just go by how my work clothes fit, so I don't have a specific number for you.\n\nDoctor: How much weight have you lost?\n\nPatient: Like I said, I don't know the exact number of pounds. But my regular doctor actually sent me here because my weight has dropped so low that everyone is making a big fuss over it. I don't see the problem, really. I honestly feel like being this light helps me keep up with the shop. It keeps me from getting slowed down when I'm working. So I don't know exactly how much I've lost, just that it's enough to make everyone else panic.\n\nDoctor: Even though you know you are thin, does your body weight feel extremely important to you?\n\nPatient: I don't really look at it like that. It's not that the weight itself is... extremely important to me. I don't sit around obsessing over it or anything. It's really just about how I feel when I'm working. I just want to stay light so I can do my carpentry work without feeling dragged down. So, no, I wouldn't say the actual weight is what matters to me.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. Never bundle several symptoms, or several attributes of one symptom, into a single question — e.g. asking whether a symptom is present and how long it has lasted at the same time.\n- If you need to ask about more than one thing, ask the one that tells you the most now and come back to the rest later.\n- Keep the question clear enough for the patient to understand.\n- Respond in English.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"intent\": \"<what you want to find out with this question>\", \"question\": \"<how you actually ask it in the interview>\"}\n"

From the dialogue so far and your candidates from the previous turn, list the disorders this patient could have.\n\n[Current Turn]\n15\n\n[Previous Candidates]\nF50.1, F50.9, R63.4\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Hello, it’s good to meet you. What brings you in today?\n\nPatient: Well, my regular doctor sent me here. Apparently my weight has dropped so low that everyone is making a big fuss about it. But honestly, I don't really see the problem. I feel like being this light actually helps me keep up with my work in the shop. It keeps me from getting slowed down on the job, you know? But they told me I needed to come in, so here I am.\n\nDoctor: Are you afraid of gaining weight?\n\nPatient: Yes, I am. If I eat anything heavy... or, you know, let myself have the foods we used to make for the holidays, I start worrying right away. I just worry that I’ll gain weight and lose the grip I’ve worked so hard to keep. It's just easier to stick to what I know.\n\nDoctor: When you look at your body, do you feel that you are still too heavy?\n\nPatient: Well, no, not exactly. I don't look in the mirror and think I'm too heavy right now. I know I'm light. \n\nIt's just... I don't want to add any extra weight and lose my edge. So I wouldn't say I feel heavy, I just want to make sure I stay exactly where I am. I just don't want to go the other direction.\n\nDoctor: Have you been deliberately eating less in order to keep your weight where it is?\n\nPatient: Yes, I have been. On the days I'm in the workshop, I usually just have tea... and maybe a few careful bites of something small. Stopping for a real meal just feels unnecessary to me. Plus, it kind of throws off the control I've got over the day, and my weight. So I just keep it to the bare minimum.\n\nDoctor: Does your body weight play a big part in how you judge yourself as a person?\n\nPatient: I wouldn't say it's how I judge myself as a person, no. I judge myself by my work, by the furniture I build... things like that. My weight is just something I manage. It's about staying disciplined and keeping that control, but it doesn't define who I am.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel fat in any part of your body?\n\nPatient: No, not at all. I don't look at my stomach or my arms and think they're fat. Like I was saying before, I know I'm light. I don't really scrutinize different parts of my body like that... it's just about maintaining the overall control. So, no, I don't feel fat anywhere.\n\nDoctor: Have your menstrual periods stopped?\n\nPatient: No, they haven't stopped... well, I mean, I went through menopause a few years ago. So I don't have periods anymore, but that's just because of my age, not because of anything else.\n\nDoctor: Have you had fainting spells?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't... I've never passed out or had any fainting spells, no. I stay pretty steady on my feet.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your current low weight is medically dangerous?\n\nPatient: No, I don't think it's dangerous. My husband and... well, the apprentices at work, they keep saying I look too thin. But I just tell them I'm fine. I really think people are just confusing discipline with sickness. I'm just disciplined. So no, I don't believe I'm in any medical danger.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever made yourself vomit to keep from gaining weight?\n\nPatient: No, I don't do that. I've never made myself throw up. I just control what I eat. I don't need to do anything like that.\n\nDoctor: What is your current weight?\n\nPatient: I'm not exactly sure right now. I don't really weigh myself... I just haven't stepped on a scale lately. I mostly just go by how my work clothes fit, so I don't have a specific number for you.\n\nDoctor: How much weight have you lost?\n\nPatient: Like I said, I don't know the exact number of pounds. But my regular doctor actually sent me here because my weight has dropped so low that everyone is making a big fuss over it. I don't see the problem, really. I honestly feel like being this light helps me keep up with the shop. It keeps me from getting slowed down when I'm working. So I don't know exactly how much I've lost, just that it's enough to make everyone else panic.\n\nDoctor: Even though you know you are thin, does your body weight feel extremely important to you?\n\nPatient: I don't really look at it like that. I don't look in the mirror and think I'm too heavy right now. I know I'm light. \n\nIt's just... I don't want to add any extra weight and lose my edge. So I wouldn't say I feel heavy, I just want to make sure I stay exactly where I am. I just don't want to go the other direction.\n\nDoctor: Have you been deliberately eating less in order to keep your weight where it is?\n\nPatient: Yes, I have been. On the days I'm in the workshop, I usually just have tea... and maybe a few careful bites of something small. Stopping for a real meal just feels unnecessary to me. Plus, it kind of throws off the control I've got over the day, and my weight. So I just keep it to the bare minimum.\n\nDoctor: Does your body weight play a big part in how you judge yourself as a person?\n\nPatient: I wouldn't say it's how I judge myself as a person, no. I judge myself by my work, by the furniture I build... things like that. My weight is just something I manage. It's about staying disciplined and keeping that control, but it doesn't define who I am.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel fat in any part of your body?\n\nPatient: No, not at all. I don't look at my stomach or my arms and think they're fat. Like I was saying before, I know I'm light. I don't really scrutinize different parts of my body like that... it's just about maintaining the overall control. So, no, I don't feel fat anywhere.\n\nDoctor: Have your menstrual periods stopped?\n\nPatient: No, they haven't stopped... well, I mean, I went through menopause a few years ago. So I don't have periods anymore, but that's just because of my age, not because of anything else.\n\nDoctor: Have you had fainting spells?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't... I've never passed out or had any fainting spells, no. I stay pretty steady on my feet.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your current low weight is medically dangerous?\n\nPatient: No, I don't think it's dangerous. My husband and... well, the apprentices at work, they keep saying I look too thin. But I just tell them I'm fine. I really think people are just confusing discipline with sickness. I'm just disciplined. So no, I don't believe I'm in any medical danger.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever made yourself vomit to keep from gaining weight?\n\nPatient: No, I don't do that. I've never made myself throw up. I just control what I eat. I don't need to do anything like that.\n\nDoctor: What is your current weight?\n\nPatient: I'm not exactly sure right now. I don't really weigh myself... I just haven't stepped on a scale lately. I mostly just go by how my work clothes fit, so I don't have a specific number for you.\n\nDoctor: How much weight have you lost?\n\nPatient: Like I said, I don't know the exact number of pounds. But my regular doctor actually sent me here because my weight has dropped so low that everyone is making a big fuss over it. I don't see the problem, really. I honestly feel like being this light helps me keep up with the shop. It keeps me from getting slowed down when I'm working. So I don't know exactly how much I've lost, just that it's enough to make everyone else panic.\n\nDoctor: Even though you know you are thin, does your body weight feel extremely important to you?\n\nPatient: I don't really look at it like that. It's not that the weight itself is... extremely important to me. I don't sit around obsessing over it or anything. It's really just about how I feel when I'm working. I just want to stay light so I can do my carpentry work without feeling dragged down. So, no, I wouldn't say the actual weight is what matters to me.\n\nDoctor: Has your doctor told you that your weight is significantly below what is considered normal for your height?\n\nPatient: Well, he didn't use those exact words, no. I mean, he obviously thinks it's too low, which is why he made me come see you. But... I don't remember him pulling out a chart or saying it was significantly below normal for my height or anything like that. He just said he didn't like where it was at, and that was that.\n\nDoctor: Has your weight become significantly lower than what is normal for your height?\n\nPatient: I mean, like I was saying earlier, I guess it must be. My doctor sent me here because my weight has dropped so low that everyone is making a big fuss over it. But I honestly feel like being this light just helps me keep up with the shop. It keeps me from getting slowed down when I'm working on my feet all day. So... yes, they think it's too low, even if I don't really see it that way.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost your appetite?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't lost my appetite. I still get hungry. I just... I don't eat very much when I do, because, like I said, I don't want to feel heavy or sluggish in the shop. But the actual feeling of being hungry is definitely still there.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. If it did not change, leave \"note\" empty.\n- If the candidates have narrowed to one and you are confident enough that no further questioning is needed, return true for is_final.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"candidates\": [\"<ICD-10 code>\", \"...\"], \"note\": \"<why the list changed, or empty>\", \"is_final\": <true or false>}\n"
  },

From the dialogue so far and your current candidates, ask the one follow-up question that best separates them.\n\n[Current Turn]\n18\n\n[Current Candidates]\nF50.1, F50.9, R63.4\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Hello, it’s good to meet you. What brings you in today?\n\nPatient: Well, my regular doctor sent me here. Apparently my weight has dropped so low that everyone is making a big fuss about it. But honestly, I don't really see the problem. I feel like being this light actually helps me keep up with my work in the shop. It keeps me from getting slowed down on the job, you know? But they told me I needed to come in, so here I am.\n\nDoctor: Are you afraid of gaining weight?\n\nPatient: Yes, I am. If I eat anything heavy... or, you know, let myself have the foods we used to make for the holidays, I start worrying right away. I just worry that I’ll gain weight and lose the grip I’ve worked so hard to keep. It's just easier to stick to what I know.\n\nDoctor: When you look at your body, do you feel that you are still too heavy?\n\nPatient: Well, no, not exactly. I don't look in the mirror and think I'm too heavy right now. I know I'm light. \n\nIt's just... I don't want to add any extra weight and lose my edge. So I wouldn't say I feel heavy, I just want to make sure I stay exactly where I am. I just don't want to go the other direction.\n\nDoctor: Have you been deliberately eating less in order to keep your weight where it is?\n\nPatient: Yes, I have been. On the days I'm in the workshop, I usually just have tea... and maybe a few careful bites of something small. Stopping for a real meal just feels unnecessary to me. Plus, it kind of throws off the control I've got over the day, and my weight. So I just keep it to the bare minimum.\n\nDoctor: Does your body weight play a big part in how you judge yourself as a person?\n\nPatient: I wouldn't say it's how I judge myself as a person, no. I judge myself by my work, by the furniture I build... things like that. My weight is just something I manage. It's about staying disciplined and keeping that control, but it doesn't define who I am.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel fat in any part of your body?\n\nPatient: No, not at all. I don't look at my stomach or my arms and think they're fat. Like I was saying before, I know I'm light. I don't really scrutinize different parts of my body like that... it's just about maintaining the overall control. So, no, I don't feel fat anywhere.\n\nDoctor: Have your menstrual periods stopped?\n\nPatient: No, they haven't stopped... well, I mean, I went through menopause a few years ago. So I don't have periods anymore, but that's just because of my age, not because of anything else.\n\nDoctor: Have you had fainting spells?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't... I've never passed out or had any fainting spells, no. I stay pretty steady on my feet.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your current low weight is medically dangerous?\n\nPatient: No, I don't think it's dangerous. My husband and... well, the apprentices at work, they keep saying I look too thin. But I just tell them I'm fine. I really think people are just confusing discipline with sickness. I'm just disciplined. So no, I don't believe I'm in any medical danger.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever made yourself vomit to keep from gaining weight?\n\nPatient: No, I don't do that. I've never made myself throw up. I just control what I eat. I don't need to do anything like that.\n\nDoctor: What is your current weight?\n\nPatient: I'm not exactly sure right now. I don't really weigh myself... I just haven't stepped on a scale lately. I mostly just go by how my work clothes fit, so I don't have a specific number for you.\n\nDoctor: How much weight have you lost?\n\nPatient: Like I said, I don't know the exact number of pounds. But my regular doctor actually sent me here because my weight has dropped so low that everyone is making a big fuss over it. I don't see the problem, really. I honestly feel like being this light helps me keep up with the shop. It keeps me from getting slowed down when I'm working. So I don't know exactly how much I've lost, just that it's enough to make everyone else panic.\n\nDoctor: Even though you know you are thin, does your body weight feel extremely important to you?\n\nPatient: I don't really look at it like that. It's not that the weight itself is... extremely important to me. I don't sit around obsessing over it or anything. It's really just about how I feel when I'm working. I just want to stay light so I can do my carpentry work without feeling dragged down. So, no, I wouldn't say the actual weight is what matters to me.\n\nDoctor: Has your doctor told you that your weight is significantly below what is considered normal for your height?\n\nPatient: Well, he didn't use those exact words, no. I mean, he obviously thinks it's too low, which is why he made me come see you. But... I don't remember him pulling out a chart or saying it was significantly below normal for my height or anything like that. He just said he didn't like where it was at, and that was that.\n\nDoctor: Has your weight become significantly lower than what is normal for your height?\n\nPatient: I mean, like I was saying earlier, I guess it must be. My doctor sent me here because my weight has dropped so low that everyone is making a big fuss over it. But I honestly feel like being this light just helps me keep up with the shop. It keeps me from getting slowed down when I'm working on my feet all day. So... yes, they think it's too low, even if I don't really see it that way.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost your appetite?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't lost my appetite. I still get hungry. I just... I don't eat very much when I do, because, like I said, I don't want to feel heavy or sluggish in the shop. But the actual feeling of being hungry is definitely still there.\n\nDoctor: If your weight were to go up, would you feel that your body had become too fat?\n\nPatient: Yes, I definitely would. If I even eat anything heavy, or... say, let myself have the foods we used to make for the holidays, I start worrying right away. I just worry that I'll gain weight and lose the grip I've worked so hard to keep. So, if my weight actually went up, yes, I'd feel like I was getting too fat.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. It keeps me from getting slowed down when I'm working on my feet all day. So... yes, they think it's too low, even if I don't really see it that way.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost your appetite?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't lost my appetite. I still get hungry. I just... I don't eat very much when I do, because, like I said, I don't want to feel heavy or sluggish in the shop. But the actual feeling of being hungry is definitely still there.\n\nDoctor: If your weight were to go up, would you feel that your body had become too fat?\n\nPatient: Yes, I definitely would. If I even eat anything heavy, or... say, let myself have the foods we used to make for the holidays, I start worrying right away. I just worry that I'll gain weight and lose the grip I've worked so hard to keep. So, if my weight actually went up, yes, I'd feel like I was getting too fat.\n\nDoctor: Before your weight got this low, were you trying to lose weight on purpose?\n\nPatient: Yes, I was. I just started cutting back on what I ate to keep things manageable. Like, on workshop days, I usually just have tea and maybe... a few careful bites of something. Stopping for a real meal just feels unnecessary to me. It throws off the control I’ve got over the day, and I guess doing that on purpose is how the weight started coming off in the first place.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. If I eat anything heavy... or, you know, let myself have the foods we used to make for the holidays, I start worrying right away. I just worry that I’ll gain weight and lose the grip I’ve worked so hard to keep. It's just easier to stick to what I know.\n\nDoctor: When you look at your body, do you feel that you are still too heavy?\n\nPatient: Well, no, not exactly. I don't look in the mirror and think I'm too heavy right now. I know I'm light. \n\nIt's just... I don't want to add any extra weight and lose my edge. So I wouldn't say I feel heavy, I just want to make sure I stay exactly where I am. I just don't want to go the other direction.\n\nDoctor: Have you been deliberately eating less in order to keep your weight where it is?\n\nPatient: Yes, I have been. On the days I'm in the workshop, I usually just have tea... and maybe a few careful bites of something small. Stopping for a real meal just feels unnecessary to me. Plus, it kind of throws off the control I've got over the day, and my weight. So I just keep it to the bare minimum.\n\nDoctor: Does your body weight play a big part in how you judge yourself as a person?\n\nPatient: I wouldn't say it's how I judge myself as a person, no. I judge myself by my work, by the furniture I build... things like that. My weight is just something I manage. It's about staying disciplined and keeping that control, but it doesn't define who I am.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel fat in any part of your body?\n\nPatient: No, not at all. I don't look at my stomach or my arms and think they're fat. Like I was saying before, I know I'm light. I don't really scrutinize different parts of my body like that... it's just about maintaining the overall control. So, no, I don't feel fat anywhere.\n\nDoctor: Have your menstrual periods stopped?\n\nPatient: No, they haven't stopped... well, I mean, I went through menopause a few years ago. So I don't have periods anymore, but that's just because of my age, not because of anything else.\n\nDoctor: Have you had fainting spells?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't... I've never passed out or had any fainting spells, no. I stay pretty steady on my feet.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your current low weight is medically dangerous?\n\nPatient: No, I don't think it's dangerous. My husband and... well, the apprentices at work, they keep saying I look too thin. But I just tell them I'm fine. I really think people are just confusing discipline with sickness. I'm just disciplined. So no, I don't believe I'm in any medical danger.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever made yourself vomit to keep from gaining weight?\n\nPatient: No, I don't do that. I've never made myself throw up. I just control what I eat. I don't need to do anything like that.\n\nDoctor: What is your current weight?\n\nPatient: I'm not exactly sure right now. I mostly just go by how my work clothes fit, so I don't have a specific number for you.\n\nDoctor: How much weight have you lost?\n\nPatient: Like I said, I don't know the exact number of pounds. But my regular doctor actually sent me here because my weight has dropped so low that everyone is making a big fuss over it. I don't see the problem, really. I honestly feel like being this light helps me keep up with the shop. It keeps me from getting slowed down when I'm working. If I eat anything heavy... or, you know, let myself have the foods we used to make for the holidays, I start worrying right away. I just worry that I’ll gain weight and lose the grip I’ve worked so hard to keep. I don't look in the mirror and think I'm too heavy right now. I know I'm light. \n\nIt's just... I don't want to add any extra weight and lose my edge. So I wouldn't say I feel heavy, I just want to make sure I stay exactly where I am. I just don't want to go the other direction.\n\nDoctor: Have you been deliberately eating less in order to keep your weight where it is?\n\nPatient: Yes, I have been. On the days I'm in the workshop, I usually just have tea... and maybe a few careful bites of something small. Stopping for a real meal just feels unnecessary to me. Plus, it kind of throws off the control I've got over the day, and my weight. So I just keep it to the bare minimum.\n\nDoctor: Does your body weight play a big part in how you judge yourself as a person?\n\nPatient: I wouldn't say it's how I judge myself as a person, no. I judge myself by my work, by the furniture I build... things like that. My weight is just something I manage. It's about staying disciplined and keeping that control, but it doesn't define who I am.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel fat in any part of your body?\n\nPatient: No, not at all. I don't look at my stomach or my arms and think they're fat. Like I was saying before, I know I'm light. I don't really scrutinize different parts of my body like that... it's just about maintaining the overall control. So, no, I don't feel fat anywhere.\n\nDoctor: Have your menstrual periods stopped?\n\nPatient: No, they haven't stopped... well, I mean, I went through menopause a few years ago. So I don't have periods anymore, but that's just because of my age, not because of anything else.\n\nDoctor: Have you had fainting spells?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't... I've never passed out or had any fainting spells, no. I stay pretty steady on my feet.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your current low weight is medically dangerous?\n\nPatient: No, I don't think it's dangerous. I'm just disciplined. So no, I don't believe I'm in any medical danger.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever made yourself vomit to keep from gaining weight?\n\nPatient: No, I don't do that. I've never made myself throw up. I just control what I eat. I don't need to do anything like that.\n\nDoctor: What is your current weight?\n\nPatient: I'm not exactly sure right now. I don't really weigh myself... I just haven't stepped on a scale lately. I mostly just go by how my work clothes fit, so I don't have a specific number for you.\n\nDoctor: How much weight have you lost?\n\nPatient: Like I said, I don't know the exact number of pounds. But my regular doctor actually sent me here because my weight has dropped so low that everyone is making a big fuss over it. I don't see the problem, really. I honestly feel like being this light helps me keep up with the shop. It keeps me from getting slowed down when I'm working. So I don't know exactly how much I've lost, just that it's enough to make everyone else panic.\n\nDoctor: Even though you know you are thin, does your body weight feel extremely important to you?\n\nPatient: I don't really look at it like that. I just want to stay light so I can do my carpentry work without feeling dragged down. It keeps me from getting slowed down when I'm working on my feet all day.
